Um... well... I mean, I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is. I'm sure it's just a temporary thing, you know? But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, because I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information... it has been a little bit of a struggle lately. 

I've been... well, I haven't been as reliable at running my division as I usually am. It's just so hard to focus on basic executive decisions when I keep getting distracted by the criticism. I think some of my senior staff have quietly started taking over some of the work I used to handle myself. I mean, they're a great team, very supportive, but I know I should be doing it. 

And at home... I feel terrible about this, but my husband has had to take on almost all the household responsibilities. I just... I start things and leave them unfinished, or I just don't start them at all because I'm so drained from the stress at work.

Um... well, it's... it's just stressful, mostly. I mean, it's not like I'm falling apart or anything! I try really hard not to let it get to me too much. Um... well, I know I touched on this a little bit earlier, and I hope it's okay if I repeat myself... but they definitely sound like they are coming from outside of me.

I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear always coming from a specific source, like the conference room walls or your cell phone, or do you ever hear them when there's complete silence and no apparent source?\n\nPatient: Well, um, like I was saying before, it really seems to mostly come from those specific places. It's... it's usually right through the conference room walls, or from my phone. And the phone thing, I know how it sounds, but I hear them even when the screen is black and it's completely turned off. It's always tied to those work things. And it just sounds so incredibly real and clear to me that I actually speak back to them. I'll answer them right out loud, and then... well, then I look around and realize no one else in the room heard a thing. \n\nI really hope I'm not making myself sound completely incompetent by telling you this. I'm usually very put-together, I promise, it's just this one weird stress thing. Did... did I answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, are they ever telling you to do something, or are they mostly just commenting on your performance?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no... they never tell me to do anything. I promise it's nothing scary like that. I'm completely in control of my actions, I really am. It's... well, like I mentioned a moment ago, it's really just them commenting on my performance. It's my staff or the board members, and they're just saying over and over that I'm ruining the company. \n\nI know I already said this, but it just comes right through the conference room walls or my phone when it's off, and it's just so incredibly clear that I end up defending myself and answering them out loud before I realize no one else heard it. Like, maybe it just proves it's all tied to my performance anxiety and being overworked? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear always coming from a specific source, like the conference room walls or your cell phone, or do you ever hear them when there's complete silence and no apparent source?\n\nPatient: Well, um, like I was saying before, it really seems to mostly come from those specific places. It's... it's usually right through the conference room walls, or from my phone. I'm usually very put-together, I promise, it's just this one weird stress thing. Did... did I answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, are they ever telling you to do something, or are they mostly just commenting on your performance?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no... they never tell me to do anything. I promise it's nothing scary like that. I'm completely in control of my actions, I really am. It's... well, like I mentioned a moment ago, it's really just them commenting on my performance. It's my staff or the board members, and they're just saying over and over that I'm ruining the company. \n\nI know I already said this, but it just comes right through the conference room walls or my phone when it's off, and it's just so incredibly clear that I end up defending myself and answering them out loud before I realize no one else heard it. But it's strictly just them criticizing my work, never telling me to do things. \n\nI hope that's a better sign? Like, maybe it just proves it's all tied to my performance anxiety and being overworked? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to figure this out... is that the kind of detail you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that hearing these voices is affecting your daily life or your ability to work and interact with others?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I mean, I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is. I'm sure it's just a temporary thing, you know? But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, because I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information... it has been a little bit of a struggle lately. \n\nI've been... well, I haven't been as reliable at running my division as I usually am. I promise I'm usually very capable, both at work and at home. It's just a rough patch, right? Did I... did I answer that okay? I feel bad because I know I shouldn't let it bother me, especially since I'm the one in charge. I should have thicker skin, right? I hope I'm not sounding too dramatic about it.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, do you feel like they are coming from inside your head or from outside of you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I know I touched on this a little bit earlier, and I hope it's okay if I repeat myself... but they definitely sound like they are coming from outside of me. \n\nLike I mentioned, it really sounds like the voices are coming right through the conference room walls, or... or projecting from my phone, even when it's completely turned off. Um... no, I don't think so. I really try to keep my thoughts organized. I mean, I have to, for my job, right? I'm always writing lists and schedules, and I think I'm usually pretty good at staying on track.

So when I hear those... those criticisms, I know it's just this strange thing happening to me right now. I know they aren't true. I've worked really hard to build a good life and a successful company, so I try to just remind myself of that. \n\nI hope that reassures you? I really don't want you to think I'm completely losing my grip or believing everything I hear. I know what's true and what isn't, I promise. I'm really trying to keep a good perspective on all of this... is that the right way to handle it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble expressing your thoughts in a coherent and logical manner when speaking to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. I actually pride myself on being a strong communicator. In my line of work, you really have to be, right? You have to be clear and logical to lead a team and run a business. So I always make a very conscious effort to speak clearly and keep everything organized when I talk to people. \n\nI... I hope I haven't been confusing you today or jumping around too much? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, it hasn't been that long, really. I'd say maybe... about three months? 

I know that sounds like a while, but it's just been these past three months that I've noticed them coming from the walls, or... or sometimes my phone. But I promise it hasn't been going on forever, and I've been managing it as best I can. I really try not to let it interfere with my work or anything. 

Is three months a long time for something like this? I hope I should have come in sooner... I just didn't want to make a fuss if it was something that would go away on its own. I'm doing the right thing by telling you now, though, aren't I?

I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear always coming from a specific source, like the conference room walls or your cell phone, or do you ever hear them when there's complete silence and no apparent source?\n\nPatient: Well, um, like I was saying before, it really seems to mostly come from those specific places. It's... it's usually right through the conference room walls, or from my phone. And the phone thing, I know how it sounds, but I hear them even when the screen is black and it's completely turned off. It's my staff, or the board, and they're just... they're saying I'm ruining the company. \n\nI don't really notice it in just random, complete silence, I don't think? It's always tied to those work things. And it just sounds so incredibly real and clear to me that I actually speak back to them. I'll answer them right out loud, and then... well, then I look around and realize no one else in the room heard a thing. \n\nI really hope I'm not making myself sound completely incompetent by telling you this. I'm usually very put-together, I promise, it's just this one weird stress thing. Did... did I answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, are they ever telling you to do something, or are they mostly just commenting on your performance?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no... they never tell me to do anything. I promise it's nothing scary like that. I'm completely in control of my actions, I really am. It's... well, like I mentioned a moment ago, it's really just them commenting on my performance. It's my staff or the board members, and they're just saying over and over that I'm ruining the company. \n\nI know I already said this, but it just comes right through the conference room walls or my phone when it's off, and it's just so incredibly clear that I end up defending myself and answering them out loud before I realize no one else heard it. But it's strictly just them criticizing my work, never telling me to do things. \n\nI hope that's a better sign? Like, maybe it just proves it's all tied to my performance anxiety and being overworked? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to figure this out... is that the kind of detail you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that hearing these voices is affecting your daily life or your ability to work and interact with others?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I mean, I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is. I'm sure it's just a temporary thing, you know? But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, because I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information... it has been a little bit of a struggle lately. \n\nI've been... well, I haven't been as reliable at running my division as I usually am. It's just so hard to focus on basic executive decisions when I keep getting distracted by the criticism. I think some of my senior staff have quietly started taking over some of the work I used to handle myself. I mean, they're a great team, very supportive, but I know I should be doing it. \n\nAnd at home... I feel terrible about this, but my husband has had to take on almost all the household responsibilities. I just... I start things and leave them unfinished, or I just don't start them at all because I'm so drained from the stress at work. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent. I promise I'm usually very capable, both at work and at home. It's just a rough patch, right? Did I... did I answer that okay? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these critical voices?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... it's just stressful, mostly. I mean, it's not like I'm falling apart or anything! I try really hard not to let it get to me too much. But, you know, when you're trying to work and you just keep hearing that you're ruining everything... it makes me feel a little anxious. Maybe a little discouraged? \n\nI just... I feel bad because I know I shouldn't let it bother me, especially since I'm the one in charge. I should have thicker skin, right? It just makes me feel a bit overwhelmed sometimes, like I'm letting everyone down. But I really am trying my best to push through it. I promise I'm not usually this sensitive. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too dramatic about it.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, do you feel like they are coming from inside your head or from outside of you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I know I touched on this a little bit earlier, and I hope it's okay if I repeat myself... but they definitely sound like they are coming from outside of me. \n\nLike I mentioned, it really sounds like the voices are coming right through the conference room walls, or... or projecting from my phone, even when it's completely turned off. It's not like a thought inside my own head, if that makes sense? It sounds so incredibly clear and real, just like you and I are talking now, which is why I end up answering them out loud before I even realize no one else is in the room. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound... well, you know, crazy. I know how strange it must sound to say my phone is talking to me when it's off, but it just feels so external to me. Did I explain that right? I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you were asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear are real?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I mean, I know how it sounds. I really do. And I want to be completely honest with you because I know you're just trying to help me figure this out. \n\nI guess... sometimes I think they must be real, because they sound so incredibly clear, like I said. But then, you know, logically... I know that phones don't talk when they're turned off. And I know people aren't really standing outside the conference room yelling at me. So... I guess I know they aren't real? \n\nI just... in the moment, it feels so real that my brain just reacts to it. But I promise I'm not totally out of touch with reality! I know it's something going on with me, and not... not actual people. I hope that's the right answer? I just don't want you to think I've completely lost my grip on things. I'm really trying my best to stay grounded. Is that... does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are always talking about you, even when you're not at work or thinking about the company?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess... it mostly revolves around my job, even when I'm not there. I hope I don't sound, you know, completely self-centered saying this... but yes, they do talk about me. \n\nEven if I'm at home and my phone is completely turned off, I'll still hear them. It's usually the board members, or some of my staff... and they're always saying that I'm ruining the company. So it's always focused on my work, even on my personal time. \n\nI try really, really hard to just leave work at the office, I promise I do! I know how important work-life balance is. But they just sound so clear that I'll catch myself answering them out loud in my own house before I realize no one else is actually there. \n\nI hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it. I just don't want you to think I'm arrogant, or that I think everyone is always obsessed with talking about me. It's just those specific people from work. Does that answer your question okay? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced anything like this before, where you heard voices or had similar thoughts that weren't based in reality?\n\nPatient: Um... no. No, definitely not. I hope you believe me, but I've never had anything like this happen before in my entire life. \n\nI'm usually very... well, very put-together and level-headed. I mean, I have to be, right? Running a company and all. So this is all completely new and... and honestly, a little embarrassing for me to even admit. I've always prided myself on being really grounded and dependable. I wouldn't have even come in if it wasn't getting so... distracting lately. \n\nI really hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with all this, or making a mountain out of a molehill. But no, I've never heard voices or dealt with anything like this before. Is that... is that helpful to know? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right background.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disjointed or hard to follow when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I really try to keep my thoughts organized. I mean, I have to, for my job, right? I'm always writing lists and schedules, and I think I'm usually pretty good at staying on track. \n\nI don't feel like my thoughts are jumbled or anything like that. I'm still able to focus on my emails and meetings when the... the distractions aren't happening. I really try my best to stay sharp. \n\nI hope that's the right answer? I just don't want you to think my brain is completely falling apart or that I can't hold a normal conversation. I feel like I'm still making sense when I talk to you... am I? I'm really trying to be clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you heard voices or had thoughts that weren't based in reality before this current experience began?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm so sorry, I think we might have just touched on that a moment ago? Or... or maybe I didn't explain myself well enough the first time, I apologize if I was confusing! I really want to be as helpful as possible. \n\nBut... no, I've never had any period in my life like this before. Never heard anything, never had thoughts that weren't real. I've always been very stable... you know, very reliable. This is the very first time anything like this has ever happened to me, I promise. I wouldn't hide anything from you, I know you're just trying to help. \n\nDoes... does that make sense? I hope I'm giving you the information you're looking for. Please let me know if I need to explain it differently!\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the criticism you hear from the voices is based on actual facts or events?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, goodness, no. No, definitely not. I really want you to know that I don't actually believe what they're saying. \n\nI mean, I know it's not based on anything real or factual. I'm a very logical person—in my position, you always have to look at the data and the actual facts of a situation, right? So when I hear those... those criticisms, I know it's just this strange thing happening to me right now. I know they aren't true. I've worked really hard to build a good life and a successful company, so I try to just remind myself of that. \n\nI hope that reassures you? I really don't want you to think I'm completely losing my grip or believing everything I hear. I know what's true and what isn't, I promise. I'm really trying to keep a good perspective on all of this... is that the right way to handle it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble expressing your thoughts in a coherent and logical manner when speaking to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. I actually pride myself on being a strong communicator. In my line of work, you really have to be, right? You have to be clear and logical to lead a team and run a business. So I always make a very conscious effort to speak clearly and keep everything organized when I talk to people. \n\nI... I hope I haven't been confusing you today or jumping around too much? If I have, I am so sorry. I know I'm a little anxious, but I really try to make sure my words come out right, even with the other things going on. I feel like I usually articulate things pretty well... do you think I'm doing okay with that right now? I just really want to make sure I'm giving you good, clear answers so you can do your job.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing these voices?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, it hasn't been that long, really. I'd say maybe... about three months? \n\nI know that sounds like a while, but it's just been these past three months that I've noticed them coming from the walls, or... or sometimes my phone. But I promise it hasn't been going on forever, and I've been managing it as best I can. I really try not to let it interfere with my work or anything. \n\nIs three months a long time for something like this? I hope I should have come in sooner... I just didn't want to make a fuss if it was something that would go away on its own. I'm doing the right thing by telling you now, though, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant stressors or major life changes in the three months leading up to when you first started hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... stressors? No, not really. I mean, nothing out of the ordinary. \n\nI've been running my company for a while now, and, you know, business is just business. There's always some level of pressure, but I handle it very well. I'm very used to it. I really haven't had any major crises or big life changes or anything like that. Everything has been pretty stable, honestly. \n\nI hope that's a good thing? I know sometimes stress can cause things to happen, but I really try to keep a good balance and not let things get to me. I've been doing a good job managing my stress, I think. Is that what you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right context.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear constant, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... they definitely come and go. It's not like a constant thing all day, every day. \n\nThey usually just pop up here and there, you know? Like... maybe when I'm alone in my office or at home. It's really not that intrusive, I promise. Most of the time, I can just focus on my work and tune it out. It's just a little distracting sometimes, but I don't let it stop me from doing what I need to do. \n\nI really hope that makes sense? I know it sounds strange, but I'm trying to be as clear as possible. I just want to reassure you that I'm still functioning really well despite it... does that sound like something that's manageable? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I'm usually very put-together, I promise, it's just this one weird stress thing. Did... did I answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, are they ever telling you to do something, or are they mostly just commenting on your performance?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no... they never tell me to do anything. It's my staff, or the board, and they're just... they're saying I'm ruining the company. \n\nI don't really notice it in just random, complete silence, I don't think? It's always tied to those work things. And it just sounds so incredibly real and clear to me that I actually speak back to them. I'll answer them right out loud, and then... well, then I look around and realize no one else in the room heard a thing. \n\nI really hope I'm not making myself sound completely incompetent by telling you this. I'm usually very put-together, I promise, it's just this one weird stress thing. Did... did I answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, are they ever telling you to do something, or are they mostly just commenting on your performance?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no... they never tell me to do anything. I promise it's nothing scary like that. I'm completely in control of my actions, I really am. It's... well, like I mentioned a moment ago, it's really just them commenting on my performance. It's my staff or the board members, and they're just saying over and over that I'm ruining the company. \n\nI know I already said this, but it just comes right through the conference room walls or my phone when it's off, and it's just so incredibly clear that I end up defending myself and answering them out loud before I realize no one else heard it. But it's strictly just them criticizing my work, never telling me to do things. \n\nI hope that's a better sign? Like, maybe it just proves it's all tied to my performance anxiety and being overworked? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to figure this out... is that the kind of detail you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that hearing these voices is affecting your daily life or your ability to work and interact with others?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I mean, I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is. I'm sure it's just a temporary thing, you know? But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, because I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information... it has been a little bit of a struggle lately. \n\nI've been... well, I haven't been as reliable at running my division as I usually am. It's just so hard to focus on basic executive decisions when I keep getting distracted by the criticism. I think some of my senior staff have quietly started taking over some of the work I used to handle myself. I mean, they're a great team, very supportive, but I know I should be doing it. \n\nAnd at home... I feel terrible about this, but my husband has had to take on almost all the household responsibilities. I just... I start things and leave them unfinished, or I just don't start them at all because I'm so drained from the stress at work. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent. I promise I'm usually very capable, both at work and at home. It's just a rough patch, right? Did I... did I answer that okay? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these critical voices?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's... it's just stressful, mostly. I mean, it's not like I'm falling apart or anything! I try really hard not to let it get to me too much. But, you know, when you're trying to work and you just keep hearing that you're ruining everything... it makes me feel a little anxious. Maybe a little discouraged? \n\nI just... I feel bad because I know I shouldn't let it bother me, especially since I'm the one in charge. I should have thicker skin, right? It just makes me feel a bit overwhelmed sometimes, like I'm letting everyone down. But I really am trying my best to push through it. I promise I'm not usually this sensitive. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too dramatic about it.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, do you feel like they are coming from inside your head or from outside of you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I know I touched on this a little bit earlier, and I hope it's okay if I repeat myself... but they definitely sound like they are coming from outside of me. \n\nLike I mentioned, it really sounds like the voices are coming right through the conference room walls, or... or projecting from my phone, even when it's completely turned off. It's not like a thought inside my own head, if that makes sense? It sounds so incredibly clear and real, just like you and I are talking now, which is why I end up answering them out loud before I even realize no one else is in the room. \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound... well, you know, crazy. I know how strange it must sound to say my phone is talking to me when it's off, but it just feels so external to me. Did I explain that right? I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you were asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear are real?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I mean, I know how it sounds. I really do. But then, you know, logically... I know that phones don't talk when they're turned off. And I know people aren't really standing outside the conference room yelling at me. So... I know it's something going on with me, and not... not actual people. I hope that's the right answer? I just don't want you to think I've completely lost my grip on things. I'm really trying my best to stay grounded. Is that... does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are always talking about you, even when you're not at work or thinking about the company?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess... it mostly revolves around my job, even when I'm not there. I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced anything like this before, where you heard voices or had similar thoughts that weren't based in reality?\n\nPatient: Um... no. No, definitely not. I hope you believe me, but I've never had anything like this happen before in my entire life. \n\nI'm usually very... well, very put-together and level-headed. I mean, I have to be, right? Running a company and all. So this is all completely new and... and honestly, a little embarrassing for me to even admit. I've always prided myself on being really grounded and dependable. I wouldn't have even come in if it wasn't getting so... distracting lately. \n\nI really hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with all this, or making a mountain out of a molehill. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right background.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disjointed or hard to follow when you're not hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. Please let me know if I need to explain it differently!\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the criticism you hear from the voices is based on actual facts or events?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, goodness, no. I'm really trying to keep a good perspective on all of this... is that the right way to handle it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble expressing your thoughts in a coherent and logical manner when speaking to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I really don't think so.
